Copy to:
GE3 (RAF)

LETTER FROM LORD HILL-NORTON ON UFOS

References:
A. MO 9/18 dated 23 May 94
B. D/Sec{AS)12/3 dated 10 May 94

1. At Reference you asked for advice on the letter from Lord
Hill-Norton concerning UFO sightings over Belgium, together with a
draft reply.

2. Lord Hill-Norton is raising concerns put to him by Mr
EEQUnROM, a UFO researcher who is very well known to this
section. first approached us in January 1993, and
my staff have now written ten letters to SERiry . I have
attached copies of this correspondence, together with copies of
the three Parliamentary Enquiries that have been generated by Mr
SEQ 's approaches to Sir Keith Speed MP. I think it is clear
that although we have done our best to answer all EERIZOME's
questions, the correspondence had reached the point where nothing
could usefully be added.

3. A detailed summary of the case is set out in D/MIN(AF)/94/94
dated 14 March 1994 (attached); essentially, a wave of UFO
sightings was reported over Belgium on 30/31 March 1990. As well
as visual sightings, some radar returns were noted, and as a
result it was decided to launch two aircraft to investigate.
These aircraft did attain some brief radar lock-ons, but as with
the returns recorded by ground based radar, this does not mean
that any structured craft was present; the point has frequently
been made in this correspondence that spurious radar returns can
be caused by a number of factors.

4, The key point in all this is that the MOD's only role as far
as UFOs are concerned is to ascertain whether there is evidence of
any threat to the defence of the UK. Given that these sightings
occurred outside the UK Air Defence Region, and given that the
Belgians found no evidence of a threat and did not inform any UK
authorities of these sightings, the whole question goes beyond our
remit.

5. Lord Hill-Norton has a long-standing interest in UFOs, and
has approached the Department on this subject before, in 1985,
when he wrote to the then § of S about a well-known UFO sighting.
He was a member of the (now defunct) House of Lords UFO Study
Group, and has written forewords for two books on the subject. He
has also given at least two television interviews on the subject.

6. Section 40 is understood to be writing a book on these
Belgian UFO sightings, and is doubtless keen to build up a large
file of correspondence to be used as part of this enterprise.

7. Notwithstanding Lord Hill-Norton's comments about a public
uproar, we do not anticipate any difficulties. The Belgian UFO
sightings are well-known among UFO researchers, and while they are
likely to be mentioned in any documentary on UFOs, they are,
effectively, old news. Lord Hill-Norton also mentioned the
demonstration against perceived government secrecy about UFOs,
held outside Main Building and Parliament on 23 May (notified to
Ministers at Reference B). This was a low key affair; there
appeared to be only around a dozen protesters, and there was
little media coverage of the event. Each MP was given a document,
which among other points mentioned the Belgian sightings; we have
yet to receive a single Parliamentary Enquiry as a result of this
lobbying.

8. The attached draft reply is self-explanatory, and has been
cleared with D Air Def's staff. Given Lord Hill-Norton's
familiarity with our policy on UFOs and with the specifics of this
particular exchange of correspondence, I have kept the draft reply
short, and avoided going into detail over points which have
already been explained in previous letters.

Sec(AS)2a
MB8245 82140MB
DRAFT REPLY TO ADMIRAL OF THE FLEET THE LORD HILL-NORTON GCB

Thank you for your letter dated 17 May concerning the UFo

sightings that occurred over Belgium in March 1990.

These UFO sightings, while clearly very interesting, are not a
matter of concern to the Ministry of Defence. You will know that
our only reason for examining reports of UFO sightings at all is
check whether or not there is evidence of any threat to the United
Kingdom. The Belgian authorities have confirmed to us that there
was no evidence of any threat; given this, and given that the
sightings occurred outside the UK Air Defence Region, the Belgians
saw no requirement to notify any UK authorities. Our own Air
Defence experts have confirmed that they would not be concerned
with reports of UFOs in the area in question. I am sure it goes
without saying, however, that any unauthorised penetration of the
UK Air Defence Region would be detected by our Air Defenders, and

dealt with as appropriate.
It is clear to me from the papers I have seen that the position
has been explained in great detail to RRQ, and I share

the view that there is nothing new to be said on the subject.

I hope this has explained the position.

23 May 1994

Dear

So sorry not to have thanked you earlier for your letter and
Blue Book enclosure, dated 20 April.

Hope the Alien Acknowledgement Campaign presentation (scheduled
to take place in a few minutes, as I write) was not too
difficult for you.

I am extremely pleased to have the Blue Book paper. You are of
course familiar with my position on this set-up, but the
following essential points need to be emphasized:

Page 1, para. 3/page 4, para. 1: UFO reports affecting
national security were never routed to Blue Book, as confirmed
by General Carroll Bolender in a USAF decument (enclosed), and
by Colonel William Coleman (who used to work for Blue Book) to
me in a recorded interview. Further, USAF Intelligence has
released a number of hitherto highly classified documents
relating to sightings (some of them Secret or occasionally Top
Secret), and many more remain classified in the interests of
national security: none of these involved Blue Book. That is
all easily proveable.

What I cannot prove is that some UFOs do indeed
“exhibit...unique scientific information or advanced technolgy
which could contribute to scientific or technical research",
although I am certain that they do and equally certain that
there is a top secret effort to understand that technology.

Brigadier General Arthur Exon, former commander of Wright-
Patterson AFB, has confirmed that fragments from the 1947
Corona, N.M. object(s) were secretly flown to WPAFB and that
lab chiefs established a special projects unit to study them.
As a lieutenant-colonel at the time, Exon flew over the crash
sites and later handled the wreckage. He told authors Kevin
Randle and Donald Schmitt that numerous tests were conducted,
involving "everything from chemical analysis, stress tests,
compression tests, flexing...The metal and material was unknown

to anyone I talked to". Exon has also confirmed that,
following Roswell, a top secret group was established which
controlled access to the wreckage, bodies, and all information
thereon, and, later, to all classified UFO reports.

In an interview in Washington for Central TV, Col. J. Fletcher
Prouty, a former USAF/CIA liaison officer - one of the five
USAF officers who established NORAD - told us that when he was
commander of a heavy transport squadron in Tokyo in 1953-4, he
received a Top Secret Eyes Only wire from AF HQ informing him
that if any of his crewmen were to see Uros they were to be
questioned separately, statements taken, and the details flown
to Washington. . Six or seven months later, 60 personnel on
board a C-54 were accompanied for over half an hour by a huge
unknown object. Needless to say, the report (like so many
others) was never seen by Blue Book officers.

it is evident that Blue Book was a very low-level and
superficial effort to study the subject, and that those
involved did not have access to the really important and
sensitive information. I am sure that similar restrictions

apply to AS2a.

Please feel free to make this letter available to your.
colleagues. I would welcome any comments.

Finally, I do hope we can get together shortly.

Dear EERIRMEN,

Supporters of AAC-ORTK Britain are today (May 23rd 1994) lobbying the MOD
and Parliament on the issue of military secrecy on the existence of UFOs.
We define UFOs in this context as intelligently operated devices of a nature
and/or technology clearly non-human, There exists an abundance of documented
evidence to fully justify a conclusion that intelligences outside that
of physical mankind are attempting purposeful interaction with mankind.

I have no way of knowing what level of awareness you personally have to
this exciting situation. I use the word exciting deliberately. It is clear
that there are many who do not view this situation as exciting. AAC-ORTK
share a view that this issue must be openly debated, and all the evidence
must be included in these public debates. This issue is for the people
and they have a natural right to know and a natural right to make their
own minds up about its implications. Please note we are not telling people
what to believe.

We have no wish to bring more fear into a world already dominated by fear.
Overcoming this basic instinct appears to be one of the first steps along
the path of integration. And public education on this subject will, we
believe, prove net beneficial to world society.

Every MP has been given a copy of the document ‘Are You Aware’ and I ask
that the MOD submit to Parliament all their documentation on the UFO issue.
Both parliamentary and public debate must begin on this subject.

Section 40 y;

RAC-ORTK Britain convener}

END UFO SECRECY NOW !

ARE YOU AWARE

That in excess of 3500 documented reports from military and civilian pilots world wide.
have confirmed the operation in the Earths atmosphere of intelligently guided UFOs, of a nature
and technology clearly non-human.

That UFOs have now lett over 4000 documented fanding traces world wide. and in
addition have generated hundreds of reports of eiectromagnetic interference of car engines.
tadios and other electrical devices.

That on the night 30th: 31st March 1990, the Belgian Air Force scrambled two F16
interceptors in response to radar images of a UFO and visual observations of the UFO reported
by civilians and confirmed by the Police. This documented interception of a purposely operated
structured UFO was one of several such encounters which occurred over Belgium between
December [989 and April [990.0n July [ith [990. Colonel W DeBrouwer, Chief of the Belgian

Air Statf made this statement:

On the night 30th and 31st March. we had an observation on the radar and in addition a
visual observation on the ground by the Police - What the pilots detected was well outside the
normal tlying envelope of an aeroplane. Sometimes they had what we cail lock-ons. which gave
a parameters varving ffom speeds between {50 knots to 990 knots. an acceleration which
occurred in a few seconds. The speeds would be impossible to tolerate for a human being, that’s
the first point. The second point is. the visual observations alwavs describe a system. a machine.
which hangs and hovers above the surtace at quite a low altitude without making anv noise. Now
with the current technology that would be impossibie™

And in the European Parliament document “Report of the Committee on Energy. Research
and Technoiogy - On the proposal to set up a European centre ror sightings of unidentified flying
objects’ (DOC EN/RR/241-2411961 Rapporteur Mir Tullio Regge had this to savin Mis paragraph
uded “M{tiitar’ Secrets’ (Page 33

“However. the Belgium Air Force savs that the Steaith Bomber was not involved in the

On page 7 he also had this to sav:

“It is not the job of Parliament to pass judgement on UFOs. on the other hand. Parliament
must take prompt steps to ensure that the information imparted to the public is correct’”

That in Mexico City on July lith 1991. during the total eclipse of the sun, tens of
thousands of witnesses observed a UFO, and 17 independently operated camcorders, at varving .
locations, recorded the event for a total of 25 minutes, Upon enhancement each camcorder had
recorded the same silver disc-shaped object. At a time when all eves would be focused on the
sky, this was undoubtedly an attempt to raise human awareness to the reality of the UFO.

That there are over 200 primary and secondary witnesses to the retrieval of a craft
evidently of non-human origin, from the New Mexico desert, near Roswell, in 1947. Many of
these witnesses gave testimony to having seen alien bodies recovered from the crash site.
Because many new witnesses are coming forward, the General Accounting Office has recently
launched a full investigation, GAO spokeswomen, Laura Kopleson said Congress Investigator
Rep. Steven Schiff has asked the GAO

“to see if there is any evidence that information regarding UFOs had been suppressed,”
following the Roswell incident.

That documentation exists on a world wide phenomenon commonly known as ‘animal
mutilations’ involving the surgical removal of genetically relevant tissues. Veterinary surgeons
have confirmed that the procedures, carried out in the fields, are un-reproducible by anv known
technology. Farmers and ranchers have testified to the existence of UFOs over fields where such
incidents have occurred. In the USA it is known that investigations into these on-going UFO/
animal interactions have been conducted by the FBI and the Governor of Colorado has spoken
publicly and officially on the matter.

These six points constitute only a small fraction of the currently available
evidence, which wholly suggests the presence of alien beings.

The public have a right to know the truth about the UFO/Alien reality.
The public now have a right to be made aware of this truth through their
representatives.

WITHOUT CONSENT

Missing Time and Abduction Phenomena:
The British Cases

By Philip Mantie and Carl Nagaitis
Foreword by Cinical Psychologist Dr lohn Shaw

WITHOUT CONSENT is the first ever Gritish investigation into a mystery which
has baffled scientists and laymen for four decades: the Abduction Phenomenon.

Traditionally linked with Ufology, the twilight science of
Ever since the phrase Flying Saucer was coined back in 1947 to describe
a sighting of a UFO in the United States, investigators have tied
to establish a link with a growing number of reports of individuals
who believe they have been carried off without consent.

Thousands of these cases have been reported world-wide,
the majority in the USA. In Britain a mere forty cases have been reported.
These cases range from individuals who have had a missing time experience
to groups of people whe allege abduction by alien beings and even visits
to spacecraft.

WITHOUT CONSENT is a compelling catalogue of mystery expeniences
that have happened to ordinary people: housewives, policemen, teachers,
businessmen. Middle-class, middle of the road, repectable citizens, ali of ther
believe they were abducted. Nene know why or by whom, although in many
of the case histories in this book they put forward their own theories to
rationalise their experience.

In its investigation of Missing Time and Abduction Phenomena,
WITHOUT CONSENT focuses on attributable experiences m the Linited Kingdom.
All the cases have been thoroughly researched and gathered together
in this comprehensive book which Jeaves the reader to decide for themselves
the futh hebind these extraordinary occurrences.

PERSONAL

The Rt Hon Malcolm Rifkind MP
Secretary of State

Ministry of Defence

Whitehall

London SW14 2HB

1? May 1994

Ay
I have been approached by a Sifted of Cranbrook, Kent, who has asked me
to help him to obtain a satisfactory response from your Ministry to an enquiry he
initiated a year or more ago, I enclose a copy of his letter to me dated 16 March 1994,

which sets out his request and his cowplaint. This is a swall part of a quite lengthy
correspondence,

He had earlier approached his Member, Sir Keith Speed, and I have seen several
letters which have been exchanged between Sir Keith and Mr Hailey and aiso your
officials, These letters do not answer BERQROMEEN'S enquiries, and he finds them
unsatisfactory. I am bound to say that I share that view, in the light of all the
circumstances.

There is no need for we to rehearse all that has already been written in these
exchanges, In short, detections were made by three NADGE radars in Germany and Belgiur
in March 1990, air defence aircraft of the Belgian Air Force were scrambled to intercept
but although the cbjects were detected and held on the radar of these aircraft as well,
no identification, or visual contact was made. There is no-dispute about these facts,
which have been confirmed by the Belgian Minister of Defence in public statements,
repeated in writing to I have advised Section 40, iaas unless the
procedure has been changed since I was Chairman of the NATO Military Committee, it is
inconceivable that the UK would not be informed (probably automatically) of a possibly
hostile, certain unidentified, detection by NADGE radars,

has been brushed off with the standard MOD response to all reports
(of which I have seen a great many) of UFO activity, which briefly put amount to "L..0.
no threat was perceived to the UK se no notice was taken or record made of the incident
wae” In this instance this has, in separate letters, been complicated by written
statements by your Ministry that no report of the Belgian detections was ever received
in the UK.

Section 40 asks, reasonably enough, "If, as Ministers assert, they had nao
knowledge of the Belgian events how could their Air Defence experts possibly conclude
that the phenomenon did not constitute a threat, as they had no knowledge of it?"

SSC

I fear that way well make a damaging public uproar about all this.
He has already had a petition to the European Parliament upheld, and his dossier has
been formally remitted to the relevant Euro Committee. A public demarche, so he tells
me, is planned for the MOD, the House of Commons, and simultaneously at the Pentagon
on 23 May. At least two television programmes in this country will carry his story
within the next few months, and this may well not be the end of it.

I strongly recommend that you should take a personal interest in having the
whole watter re-examined, so that a wore satisfactory and convincing reply may be
given to Sites) 'S question, before the matter gets out of hand,

L6th March 1994.

Dear Admiral Lord Hill-Nortan

I have been informed or the tollowing facts in a letter dated 26th May
1993 trom Leo Delcroix the Belgian Minister of Defence.

That on the 15th and i6th December 1949, radar detections were made by
Semmerzeke (Belgium? and Yedem(Germany) of tiying objects over Eupen in
Easteru Belzium

On the night of 30/31st March 1990,detections were made by Semmerzeke
and Glons CRC(NADGE) radars of the Belgian Air Force,and Vedem in
Germany,of an apparent airbourne intruder in the vicinity of Brussels in
Belgium. Aircraft of QRA J Wing of the Belgian Air Force were involved.
These detections followed visual observations by many thousands of
witnesses,of whom 75 were members of the Belgian Gendermerie.

A report was received dated 15th August 1993, It was made by Major
Lambrechts ¥S/3 Ctl-Met 1 of the Belgian Air Force. It states that the
Belgian Air Force scrambled two F~16 interceptors ‘Nos AL 17 and AL 23)
from the base at Bevekon,

These tighters were vectored to their targets by Glons CRC NADGE radar.
This incident caused a major security alert. It lasted for over 1 hour.
At 22h 39m an interception occurred during which radar ‘lock-on' was
acheived by both aircraft, Thie lasted tor 45.9 secs.

At this time the target was travelling on & Westerly heading towards UK
airspace at speeds in excess or 1000 knots.

The Belgian Minister ot Detence at that time Guy Coeme,stated in the
Eelgian Parliament on the 2lst December 1989 that;- ‘All hypotheses can
be excluded, the Minister could not tell what these flying objects were.'

A report has been submitted to the European Parliament for an enquiry
into these incidents. 1t is No 990/95. It has been accepted for
consideration by the Petitions Committee.

NATO and the Belgian agencies have provided conclusive proot of these
detections. How is it that I was intormed by BEQRMRIM or the Ministry
of Defence in a Letter D/SectASi12/3 of 13th October 1993, that there is
ho record in the UK of thie incident and,despite the track having come
to within six minutes of Kent,it could not therefore have been
considered a threat to the security ot the UK?

Your comments would be appreciated

Yours sincerely,

Section 40

Valencia, April 18, 1794.

Dear BER

Your letter of & April has been gratefully received.

Regarding information on the process of declassification of the
UFO archives by the Spanish Air Force, please find enclosed the
following documents:

1. My paper "Spanish Air Force UFO Files: The Secret’s End", a
lecture I delivered in a symposium in the United States last
summer. It shows the history behind the decision, steps given
and methodology.

2. A summary article entitied "The Spanish Air Force UFO Files".

3. A very recent interview to Lt General
Commander-in-Chief of MOA (4erial Operative Command, organism
which is the responsible of UFO matters now), where he replies
one question on the UFO deciassification process.

4. File 741124, Canary Islands. Copy of one example of
declassified UFO file from the official archives.

Up to now, 37 files have been fully declassified, covering the
period August 1762 to August 1975, with over S00 pages of
material disclosed.

There is a lot of additional details to say about this process,
one to which I am specially linked. Do not hesitate to ask, in
the future. Eventually, if there is enough interest, I might
fly to London to brief you in dekail.

AAS LETTERS MULLUFOL

Notes made at 2250 BST (2150 GMT) Fri 15 Oct 1993

Bygsection 40 |
section 40 RE Scction 40 [aa ESection 40 |

At about 2240 BST this evening my wife Alison and I were in bed reading at
the above address, which is at about 56 21.5N, 5 52.5 W. She suddenly got
out of bed and ran into the bathroom next door, then called to me to come

and join her.

I ran into the bathroom and she showed me through the window an irregular
flashing light in the sky to the SSE at about 8 deg. elevation. At first
I thought it was an aircraft strobe, but the colour was an intense blue-white
and the rhythm was irregular. There would be two flashes close together,
_ then a pause, the cycle being repeated about once every second. Also the
light appeared to be skittering about its mean position, a sort of Brownian
‘motion, while moving steadily to my left and downwards in elevation.

I ran downstairs to the front door, followed by BEQIRME,O to get a view
unhampered by the glass of the bathroom window. The house faces SE, and
stands a short distance from the north shore of Loch Buie, an inlet in the
south coast of Mull. It was a cold, clear night, almost cloudless, no moon,
but stars visible and still enough daylight to make out the skyline of the
Laggan peninsula which forms the SE shore of Loch Buie, about 3 miles away
and rising to about 1000 feet.

This is an important detail, because it enabled me to establish that the
'Brownian' motion, or skittering about, was NOT an optical illusion. We
both watched as the object moved across the sky until it was on a bearing
of 130 True, which is the centreline of the house, and just above the
skyline of the Laggan peninsula. For several seconds, the light danced
about the horizon, disappearing below it and then reappearing, before

its final disappearance.

During the few minutes that we watched it before its disappearance at
2248, I moved further from the house and my eyes adapted more to the
dark. I could then see that after the two main flashes, there were a
succession of faster, dimmer ones, before the cycle repeated.

The angular velocity of the object was about the same as a passenger jet
at high altitude, and we are familiar enough with these being under one
of the main flight paths from London and Europe to N. America for both
civilian and military air traffic. (If the Lockerbie flight had taken
off on time, the bomb would have gone off over us).

- BERMEN Gold me that her attention had been attracted by a noise which she
could not describe, and it was this that had caused her to run to the
bathroom window. I did not hear anything, but am slightly deaf so could
well have missed it. At no point did I hear any sound that might have been
attributable to the object. It was a quiet night with calm sea and light

northerly wind.
I wrote this down immediately after the event, returning to check for any
reappearance at intervals but saw none. There was some cirrus cloud to the
south, but otherwise the sky clear and Milky Way visible in a band almost
directly overhead.

Gin aid she had seen a cluster of lights behaving similarly when working
on her own at a house near here on a winter evening, 2 - 3 years ago.

She also said that the noise she had heard might have been a car on the
shore road below the house, about 150 yards away, and she had got up to
look because there shouldn't be anyone driving along there at that time of
night. We have in the past been bothered by hippies, religious nuts and _
other undesirables camping down there and that was why she investigated.
There was no vehicle.

NOTES ADDED SAT. 8 APR. 1994

My wife reminded me of this episode last night, causing me to have second
thoughts about it. At the time, I had decided. that it was a close formation
of military aircraft, with blue strobe lights flashing at slightiy different
rates and ‘intensities.

Then I thought: what known aircraft have intense blue-white strobes?
Amber is the usual colour, for better visibility; blue

is bad for this.

and: what pilots are going to fly long distances at night in
close formation? (1 fly light aircraft myself).

Reconstructing the line taken in the sky by the object(s) and assuming an
altitude of 35,000', I obtained a rough heading of NW -> SE, speed 400 mph,
and a formation size of less than 1000'. I had estimated the diameter of

the light-cluster at 1/4 degree, half the full moon. This is a very close
formation indeed. Is this what it was, or was it a single object with a large
number of flashing lights on it? Decided to write to ATC at Prestwick.

by PAUL MUNGO

Myth wortd: to
beliove in angels
and crop circles

would take a miracle —

Extrasensory
deception

The occult? It’s a load of crop circles

once interviewed a man who claimed.

to have been kidnapped by aliens and

taken away ina flying saucer. 1 don't

rethember the details, but | think they
had performed unspeakable medical experi~
ments on him, then whisked him off to their
home planet in the star system Whatnot. A
few months later, he says, he was deposited
back by his car on the lonely road where the
adventure had begun.

This man had subsequently written a book
about his ordeal, which, because it purported
to be fact rather than fantasy, sold racher well,
It was even made into a movie, and ac the
time he was planning to write a sequel. The
aliens, he said, had returned,

He related all this in a deadpan, almost
monotone voice. He never smiled, never
acknowledged for even an instant that he was
play-acting and that you knew he was, and
that he knew you knew it, and you knew he
knew you knew... The price he paid for his
book sales was a few interviews with queru-
lous, cynical journalists, so he played his role
and, ultimately, he banked the cheques.

PHOTO LIBRARY

is possible, 1 suppose, thar hé was indeed
abducted from his car by aliens, endured
extraterrestrial medical experiments, visited
the star system Whatnot, and chat I've entirely
misjudged the man, But somehow | doube it.

For a start, 1 don’t believe in this stuff. |
don't believe in UFOs, ley lines, astrology, the
paranormal, faith healers, premonition,
witches, forcune tellers, the occult, or any
explanation of crop circles other than natural
@ phenontena, t tend, in fact, to agree with the

DEL CROCIFERI, VENICE, THE BRIDGEMAN ART LIBRARY; SC:ENCE

ORATORIO

Ofcourse, I know, it coutd have been true. It -

mony can establish a “miracle” unless it
is of such a kind that its falsehood would
be more astonishing than the fact it is meant
to establish. In other words, if someone tells
you he’s been up in a flying saucer, or insists
that he saw a statue of the Virgin Mary mov-
ing about, you need only consider one ques-
tion: is his story more plausible than the pos-
sibilicy he’s mistaken? [n dealing with the
paranormal, or the occult, or flying saucers,
the premise is so fintastic dhac the simpler
explanation — chat your informant is either
misguided, or a lunatic, or, more likely, lying
through his teeth —is almost certainly correct.

In the case of my interviewee, the fact chat
prior to his “kidnapping” he had been a none-
too-successful writer of pulp fiction, and that
since then he had become a best-selling
author, is reason enough to query his story.
Which do you find easier to believe: that he
had been kidnapped by aliens, or that he had
founda simple way co make a fast buck? QED).

Yee a substantial number of people would
still choose co believe that he had been
abducted by alien beings. The simple fact is
that six years short of the second millenium,
despite the extraordinary advances of science
and technology, we seem to'be experiencing
a resurgence in superstition — in beliefs chat
should, logically, have died out centuries ago.

I was recently senca book from a reputable
publisher purporting to provide “authoritive
evidence that the ‘strange but true” phenom-
enon is alive and well amid the hi-tech
Nineties". Among these exciting manifesta-
tions of the paranormal were “premonition,
thoughtography, photokinesis... faich heal-
ing and other phenomena linked to physical
inanifestations” as well as cases of people
“who can switch on and off street lighting at
will”, Street lighting? 1 know that trick. [
once had a friend in Paris who lived across
the Seine from the Eiffel Tower. At nine at
night (or whenever it was) he would gather
everyone on his patio, clap his hands dramat-

stupidity (the position of the stars at the
moment of your birth have absolucely no
effect on your personality — honest), ocher-
wise sensible people persist in crediting it
with ac least some validity. It has none, and [
groan every time I'm asked my star sign
(Gemini, as a matter of fact. Of couse, you
knew...) Also, religious mysticism is back in
fashion; quasi-Christian occultism (mixed
with a lite New Age banality) has under-
gone a quict renaissance in popular mychol-
ogy. A new book, called Augels: True Stories of
How They ‘Pouch Our Lives, a collection of
peoples accounts of being helped by angels, is
to be published later this year. Another book,
The Second Coming: a Terrifying The Story,
purports to be “a spine-chilling account of
the occult, focusing on the relationship
between psychics, ritual landscapes and com-
munication with arcane spirit guardians and
varth energies”. The author's previous book,
apparently, demonstrated that the hurricane
of October 1987 (and probably the collapse of
the world’s stockmarkets too) wis an occule
plot concocted by someone called the Black
Alchemist to pave the way for the Antichrist.
Obviously, there's a market for this stuff —
otherwise publishers wouldn't peddle it. Why
this should be so in 1994 is another question:
it’s been suggested that science has become so
arcane that ordinary people, like you and me,
have been left behind. We console ourselves
with the certainties of pseudo-science, like .
astrology, or the occult, or mysticism. Or per-
haps we're frightened by reality, so we look
for miracles, manifestations of a wiser benef-
icence, represented in earlier times by: reli-
gious wonders and these days by UFOs.
When David Hume was writing, it was
during a period when scientific rationalism
was vying with (and replacing) popular
superstition. Were he somehow to come
back now, he would find it odd that many of
those things he thought would be swept away
into the dustbin of mythology were still

the area which are being carefully
studied. The group hag also joined

HUDDLED conspiratorially in a. quiet corner of

The Green Gace pub in Smallbridge, near

Rochdale, I fee! Iam on the receiving end of

some highly privileged information as it’s being

delivered in decidedly hushed tones, As Mike

Jones opens his briefcase to bring out a wad of

literarure supporting his cheories, ] glance around

furtively just in case there’s a camera recording

(our Conversation. This may sound rather MIS,

but the subject matter is nothing short of out-

landish.

For the past 27 years, Mike Jones has been a
dedicated “ufologist” although he prefers the
term “UFO researcher and investigator.” A paost-
man by day, Mike spends every spare moment
writing articles, giving talks and co-ordinating
the Rochdale UFO Research Bureau. Totally
comtnitted to the pursuit of the “ruth” whatever
that tay reveal, his main motivation is to rid the
topic of all its comical connotations.

“Basically, 1 find myself in a no-win situation.
On the one hand we have a government who
doesn't want the public co know what's going on.
On the other hand, we have the public who say
they want to know, but as soon as you tell chem,
you see this fear curtain drop down. It’s what psy-
chiatrists call the “denial factor.” People are not
only afraid of what UFOs mean but they're afraid
of being laughed at.”

Mike's fervent enthusiasm for UFO research
has earned him a reputation as one of the leading
experts in the north west, but still his friends at
work call him “The Spaceman.” His fascination
was first inspired by a schoolboy love of astron-
omy. It was while skywatching that he had his
first UFO sighting.

“A friend of mine had a telescope and we saw
what could only have been an unidentified flying
object changing direction at right angles. T know
it wasn’t an aircraft and it was in the days before
the first sputnik was launched.

“There was nobody I could turn to. My grand-
father patted me on the head and said | had an
overactive imagination. P’ve had sightings since. I
always feel tremendously proud when it happens.

? Sometimes bright lights just zoom up and stop
; dead as if caunting yau. I know the night sky like
the back of my hand and if I can’t explain it, it’s
; unexplained.

> “I never go looking for UFOs. I have a saying,

‘LE they want you, they know where you are.’
F ‘There's no point in seeking them out.”

Which brings us along to one of Mike's more
unsettling theories. On the night that he had a
sighting of three revolving red lights on the way
to (nor from, } hasten ta 2dd) the local pub, he
‘ believes that a friend of his was kidnapped by
_ aliens near to a motorway service station on the
> M62. This notion is probably harder co swallow

Having only just discovered I am pregnant

“The medical profession are closing ranks. In

Reading an extract from a copy of his UFO
magazine, I discover that as a result of the
“Kalahari Incident” in 1989, two EBEs (extra-ter-
restrial biological entities, American jargon for
alien) were discovered in a disc-like object which
had crashed in ue desert. Phe description went as
follows: “Four feet tall, greyish blue skin, oversized
head, prominent cheekbones, slanted eyes, thin
neck, no ears, short thin legs, chest and abdomen
covered in scaly ribbed skin, webbed hands and
feet with three digits, cotally devoid of body hair
and no exterior sexual organs.” Not the sort of
person you'd like to meet in a dark alley.

“What everybody wants is evidence,” stresses -
Mike, “and there we have it! Photographs. Bodies.
Recovered alien craft. What more can you want?

“Strangely enough, the Kalahari Incident hasn’t
hit the headlines here. We are so far behind the
rest of the world. This government is the most
secretive in NATO.”

Closer to home than the Kalahari desert, there
have been spates of UFO sightings around the
Rochdale/Todmorden area and I wonder if Mike
can throw any light on them?

“Tr could be something as straight-forward as lay
Hines - the Earth’s natural energy lines. There was
a sighting in the centre of Heywood in 1988,
Whatever ir was split into three and had police
cars chasing it all over the place. Over near
Warrington I know a man who had a paranormal
experience. He saw a robot-like figure wearing a
silver suit walk down an embankment and dissolve
through a chain mesh fence, It stopped his watch
and fused his car radio together. It was St Patrick’s
Night so you can imagine what the police said.”

If he was financially able, Mike would gladly
devote all his time to the UFO cause.

“I would like to make my living out of this bur

New group to look
into UFO sightings

I THOUGHT that you may be interested to learn of
“the existence of our organisation, which was estab-
lished last year in response to the recent upsurge in
sightings in this region.

Our prime concern is the investigation and identifica-
tion of anomalous aerial phenomena, and we endeavour
to work with any objective source to achieve that uins.

Although SURFORIN is an independent organisation,
we work closely with other national groups, sharing our
research findings with both the British UFO Research
Association (BUFORA) and Quest International.

- We have also established contacts at the Ministry of
Defence, and often.call upon their expertise in the identi-
fication of eatellite re-entries, location of weather bal-
loons, ete.

Obviously it i is crucial to our existence that we are told
of any UFO sightings in the Somerset area; therefore my;
primary reason for writing is to ask if people could con-
‘tact us should any phenomena of this nature occur.

ds return we will be pleased to offer our assistance and
advice. .

The Defence Press Office (RAF)
Room 0393

Main Building,

The Ministry of Defence
Whitehall SwW1

March 30 1994
REF: Project Uro

pear SEIZE.

We are currently producing a documentary on the subject of UFO
phenomena. This is a serious and in-depth presentation of various
aspects focuesing chiefly on the objective reality of reports and

witness accounts.

Much emphasis is given to reliable accounts from military and
scientific personnel. We have recently returned from the United
States and Russia where our interviews have included statements
from Colonel Fletcher Prouty (one of the architects of the NORAD
defence system) and Congressman Steven Schiff. In Russia we also
filmed interviews with Colonel General Igor Maltsev (Deputy Chief
of Btaff of the Soviet Air Force) and Colonel Boris Sokolov who
headed a ten year military and scientific projact into the topic

in the 1980's,

Another aspect of the programme includes brief sequences from the
public at large who have captured unusual aerial phenomena with

their camcorders.

We are aware that there is a significant gap in terms of

statementa from official British sources, and although we are also
talking to Lord Peter Hill-Norton, former Chief of the Defence
Staff, wa would like to include an interview with ZUNE.
your spokesman on these matters.

We believe it is important that the British public are made aware
that this department provides 4 focus for those who have witnessed
or indeed filmed unusual aerial phenomena. As 2 public service we
have found this is relatively unique and worth emphasising.

DAVENTRY EXPRESS
-Northants-

47 MAR 1994

Spooky goings on in Church Stowe

A SPOOKY tale or sclence fact, Penetrating the Web, heid here by Elsle Oakensen, may

have some of the answers.

A CHURCH Stowe woman
who spotted a UFO has had

her experience immortalised -

on video.

Elsie Oakensen’s trip
home from Daventry to the
village one May day in 1978
has left her o minor celebrity
in UFO circles.

Her appearance in the
video, Penetrating The Web,
is the latest in a long line of
screen spots, radio shows and
mentions in at least ten books
‘on the subject.

“It’s very flattering to still

be invited to go on these pro-
grammes,” said Elsie, who
never gets tired of telling her
story.

Her journey home to
Church Stowe went wrong as
she turned right at Weedon
crossroads, where she first
noticed a grey dumbbell
object with red and Breen
lights.

As she entered the village
her car came to a halt.

“By the schoolrooms I was
in complete darkness except
for'a series of white lights

(34 tar 42D)

with no beam,” she
explained. After the lights
had circled around for a time,

left, and Elsie: found her-
ne, driving along the road as
before, only her journey had
taken twice as long as it
should have.

A few months later Etsie
heard of a group of four
Byfield women travelling

Pensioner’s
UFO sighting
‘confirmed’
by second

report

Pensioner Alfred Clarke thought
he was imagining things when he
saw a UFO from his kitchen win-
dow — until he read the Oxford
Mail, sister paper of the Bicester
Advertiser.

~ For there in black and white
was Geoff and Jenny Cunning-
ham's account of their sighting of
a UFO on the same night.

The. Cunninghams from Hamp-
den Avenue, Thame, saw.an 80f
long object near the Oxfordshire
Golf Club at Moreton on the edge
of the town.

Mr Cunningham said it was
about 25ft high, with lights round
the middle, and emitted an orange
glow. It appeared to go down near
Kidlington airfield.

Mr Clarke, 69, of Nuffield Close,
Bicester, said he saw a huge ob
ject, glowing red, from his kitchen
window as he made a cup of tea
one evening.

He said it was in the sky for a
few minutes then disappeared be-
hind a house. It was heading
north.

Since the sighting he has been
teased by his family.

He said: “I was as sober as a
judge. I don't drink. But when I
told my son he said I was crazy.

“But when I saw it in the paper
I realised 1 was not talking out of
my hat and I was quite sane.”

No-one at the Oxfordshire Golf
Club or Kidlington airfield had
any knowledge of the machine
and no sightings were reported to
Thames Valley Police.

WAS it a bird? Was it a plane? Or

Two friends are trying to unravel the
mystery of a series of flashing lights they:

Andrew Wragg, aged 25, was walking to a phone
box to ring for a taxi after a night out with friends ;
when he saw a strange light in the sky.

He said: “I saw a flashing light, then a strip of :
lights like you'd see on a fairground ride. The-* +
lights, were flashing across a distance of about: -

Baffled by what he was seeing, Andrew dashed
back to his friend Shane Dowles’ house.

Burngreave, and his wife Elsa went outside and
also witnessed the strange phenomenon.

“If someone had told me about the lights T

Stadium or the Arena, but they were different
from anything down there," added Andrew.

“We couldn't see a shape behind the lights. We'd
just to know if anyone else saw the lights or know

Andrew and Shane saw the Jights at 10.10pm on
Wednesday night.

- Dundee Courier & Advertiser

12MAR 1994

AVING SPENT many years tracking Sandy

Kidd’s efforts to invent a space machine, I was
intrigued to learn that the European Parliament now
tntends to establish a UFO observatory in France.

Not surprisingly, this news has gone down in-
certain quarters like a lead balloon with Teddy Taylor,
MP, describing it as “a madcap proposal...an idea
cooked up in some kind of ethereal dream world.”

Perhaps so. But millions of people have reported
seeing strange .objects.in the sky, with many eye

witness accounts of space ships janding_and.contact—.
‘being made With théir occupants, When such stories

hit the headlines there usually follows a bout of flying

saucer mania, .
I was ‘caught up in such a mood six years ago in
Australia where T had gone to report on laboratory
tests being carried out on the controversial Kidd
Machine. i was staying with the Dundee engineer and
his wife on a bungalow estate just outside Melbourne.
One morning I was wakened early by a loud, eerie
humming noise approaching the house, until I was
certain a space craft was hovering overhead.

At that time the ‘Australian media was - givin
extensive coveragé to a family's graphic account o:
how thelr car had been buzzed by a UFO while driving
across the Nullarbor Plain, east of Adelaide. For the
next few minutes I was convinced this space invader
had returned, this time to carry off Sandy Kidd and his
antl-gravity device. :

This, [ know, sounds a laughable, far-fetched theory
now, but at the time, in the half light of that otherwise

eaceful morning, I really did believe the Martians
Fad arrived. Peeping through the curtains I saw a
large vehicle adorned with flashing yellow lights
moving slowiy down the street. Uniformed men were
darting silently up and down the driveways as if
looking for a particular address.
’ Then suddenly the truth dawned. | was witnessing
nothing more than the cleansing department on their
. weekly round, the men emptying wheelie bins into the
rear of 8 mobile crushing unit. I crept back to bed,
never so glad to have seen the scaffies. °°:
Ever since, | have kept an open mind on extra-
terrestrial phenomena, although one thing does
_ puzzle me. ¥
‘by individtials or small groups of observers?
doesn't one of these flying saucers float at .200 feet
above the centre of Dundee during the rush hour so

that everyone can witness the miracle for themselves? °

Putting that point to a UFO buff this week, he
replied, “That would never happen. The crews of
these space craft know that low flying over built-up
areas is against our laws. You saw what happened to
the paraglider who landed recently on the roof of
Buckingham Palace. He finished up in court, Our
friends from outer space wouldn’t want that.”

“Ah well, now we know.

2 TEN By Dana’ MILBANK *
. . Staff Reporter .
LONDON ~ These -are- weird times. In
fact, the times are a full 3.5% weirder than:
they were Just a yearago.
That, at least, is the conclusion of the
_ Fortean Times, a London-based magazine :
dedicated to the study of all things bizarre.
The February/March issue of the 20,000-cir- :
culation journal compares thousands of -
‘zany happenings in 1992 “a! “.7.1993, and j
declares; somewhat arbitrarty, that the ;
overall strangeness index rose ‘during the ,
period to 3,520 from 3,400. Among the |
curiosities of 1993: .

Ticket, Please

A trash bin belonging to the London
borough of Lewisham was found beside the
Sea of Galilee.'An enormous egg laid by an
extinet elephant bird washed ashore in
Australia. Sixty lambs in Germany were
attacked and killed by hundreds of crows.
‘Swedish doctors cured a deaf man by
removing a 47 “year-old bus ticket from his
ear.

The Fortean Times Index {not , ‘to be
| confused with the.Financial.Times Index, .
which has been heading the other way), has
34 components, including apparitions,

-rise so-far, Mr. Rickard has also recorded a

electrical device used to stun fish. Another .

! wrench stuck on his. testicles.

strange things falling from the sky and
water monsters. In each category, the
editors pondered their newsclippings from
30 countries to gauge the change in weird:
ness if 1993.

‘Leading the index upward was the

Strange Behavior component, which in-

.Cludes people whe throw birds into cars

waiting at stoplights, people seriously: in-
jured by vegetables ejected from passing
cars,--and .the robber. who taped two
cucumbers together and pretended he hada
sawed-off shotgun. .

The Hoaxes and Panics category gota
boost from Chong Qing, China, where peoplé
were convinced that a giant deranged robot
from America was killing and sucking the
blood of people who wore red.

“People are ‘more and more erratic,”
Says Robert J.M, Rickard, the editor.
“There are just such stupid extremes of
behavior.” an

~~ The component for Out of Place Animals
also had strong gains in 1993 on the
humerous citings of pumas and panthers
around Britain. But the Manimal compo-
‘nent, which includes Bigfoots, declined in
1993, ‘They may be becoming extinct,” Mr. .
Rickard - theorizes. “Or people may be
sobering up.” The index also saw a dip in

- The new FT Index hasn’ ‘t exactly m moved
‘the market. ‘I salute these guys, and I'm
“intrigued | to think the world is becoming less
-Tational, ‘but it's probably worthtess," says «
Nicholas Stevenson, an &.G. Warburg & Co.
European equity strategist. ‘If people start
using it for investment analysis, that would
be even more weird. " . :

Spanner in the Works :

” There are early signs that 1994. may be
even weirder than 1993. Sightings of the
‘Virgin Mary and highway ghosts are on the.

surge in crimes against men’s genitals after
the celebrated ‘case in Virginia. A Chinese
woman cut off her husband's penis in the
belief that he would grow a new one and it -
would restore their marriage. A Filipino
woman wired her.-man's penis ‘to. an-

man-came to a hospital with.a monkey

“With such weirdness already on record -
for.1994, Mr. Rickard thinks his FT Index
could well outperform the other FT Index..
For the Fortean Times, ‘at least, that’s a |
bullish sign.

“The world is more full of anxiety, a
more uncertain place,” Mr. Rickard says.
“I take comfort in the fact thatit’s getting
weirder. Il shows that man hasn't explained
away everything yet:"

U.F.0. Sighting
Garforth, Leeds.

Date
25/3/94

Time
9.35 p.m.

Researcher

Section 40

Beeston
Leeds Slaten

Tel:

At 9.45 p.m. on Friday 25th March 1994, I received a telephone call from
a gentleman in Garforth, Leeds. He reported seeing three strange lights
and an orange ball shooting northwards over Leeds. Due to the gentlemans
position in society he wishes to remain anonymous. What follows is his
own written report of the sighting.

Looking towards Leeds in the constellation of Orion, I noticed a bright
flashing light travelling northwards at a relatively high speed, I then
noticed another bright flashing light at approximately 30° up and to
the north of the first light. Its movement equating the first.

I then looked around to see if this was occurring elsewhere in the sky,
to the east I noticed a third white flashing light which after 2-3 seconds

vanished.

At this point I called my mother out from the house to see this, I was
pointing this out when just above the horizon I noticed an orange light

travelling fast, much faster than the other lights. I also pointed this
out to my mother, after it passed out of sight no more lights were seen.

While this was happening there was absolutely no noise whatsoever, even
from the roads around Garforth.

All tights described were travelling far faster than a normal aircraft.

It is possible that the white lights were chasing or trying to follow
the orange light or were watching from a distance. Also possible that
the lights were on approach to Leeds Bradford Airport.

A conversation the following day with a family friend revealed that he
had also seen the strange lights at around the same time from his home
in Castleford, this gentleman declined to give an interview.

Following my leads I telephoned Leeds Bradford Airport, what follows
are questions and answers discussed over the telephone.

Q. "I am researching a U.F.O. sighting in Garforth, Leeds on the 25th
March 1994, have you any other report of this?"

A. “Apart from the one you are researching, no, sorry."

Q. "I understand that an aeroplane had just landed at around the sighting
time, could you confirm this?"

A. “Yes, it was a D.C.9 from London and landed at 9.30 p.m."

Q. "J also understand that an aeroplane was due to land, can you confirm
this also?"

A. "Yes, that was a 757 from Alacante and Tanded at 9.40 p.m."
Q. “Did the flight crew on these aircraft have anything to report?"
A. "Sorry, no."

Telephone conversation ended.

I also telephoned the R.A.F. Base at West Drayton, what follows is the
telephone conversation.

Q. "I am researching a U.F.0. sighting in Leeds on the 25th March 1994,
could I have your name please?" :

A. Refused to give personal name but did say "We are A.I.S. Military."
Q. “Could I have your extension number for future reference?"
A. "Yes, it's EEMn

Q. "Have you had any reports of this U.F.0. sighting or any reported
sighting in the Leeds area on the 25th March 1994 at around 9.35 p.m,?"

A. "No."

Q. "Could this sighting have been military air traffic or experimental
aircraft?"

A. "No idea, we don't research these reports, you will have to contact
M.0.D."

Q. "Can you confirm that a NATO training exercise had taken ptace on
the 25th March 1994?”

A. “Sorry, no, I can't confirm that."

Conversation ends.

One thing has left me puzzled about this conversation with the young lady
at West Drayton Air Force Base, when I introduced myself and told her

why I was calling she said, "Hang on while I get an Official Report Form".
She then answered one of my questions, "We don't research these reports".

If no research into these sightings is done by the military, why would
they need an Official Report Form!

With reference to your recent letter concerning a

Sorry we could not be of more help,

Dear Beet)

Io wish to thank you for your kind letter and informations dated
25 August 1993 regarding the wave of UFO sightings reported in
the early hours of March 31, 1993.

Io want to correspond to you by providing the firm conclusions
of my research on this issue. These represents the scientific
consensus among international aerospace centers all aver the
world.

Gn March 30, 1993 (12:00 Z hours) the Russian ELINT satellite
Cosmos 2238 was launched. Goddard Space Flight Center's
publication Spacewarn Bulletin, April 1993, reports that Cosmos

2238 rocket booster decayed on March 31, 1993 (Attachment 1).

NASA associated the fiery objects with the Cosmos 2238 rocket
body reentry, which was predicted to fall at O1820 Z plus/minus
two hours. (Attachment 2),

NORAD provided the ground trace (Attachment 3). In addition to
the predicted course, it included the actual position of the RE
at 23:56 Z (on March 30) and its orbit, which was to cross a
few minutes later over Ireland, Britain, France and the
Mediterranean sea. They received a report of a visual sighting
over British isles at 00:06:43 Z (on March 31). (Attachment 4).
Apparently the decay took place one orbit before predicted
time. The Cosmos 2238 orbital path was NW-SE crossing Wales,
France and the Mediterranean sea. There were lots of "UFO"
sightings over the southwest of Britain, at around ©OoO:10 Z, as
you know well, yet there were numerous sightings also all over
France and the northeast of Spain at the interval O0:10-00:14 Z,

The three-country "wave" path correlate perfectly well with the
Cosmos orbit, (Attachments S and &). Qualitatively speaking,
the descriptions of sightings fully match with this space
debris reentry too.

Tne CNES (French NASA) released a formal announcement
explaining the observations as the Cosmos 2228 Cyclone rocket
decay (Attachment 7).

Prompted by beliefs more than by facts, and prefer to believe
that a "fleet of flying saucers" were crossing Europe that

finding an accurate explanation of the events. :

Feel free to use the enclosed material as you determine,

Thanks once more for your gratifying correspondence. I hope toa
be in touch again with you someday in the future. Before I
close, I might point out that I played an instrumental role in
making the Spanish Air Force decide to declassify its official
UFO files (the actual declassification process started in
1992). If you would like to receive additional information on

this topic, please let me know.

Le CNES étudie un phénoméne de rentrée atmosphérique
observé en Frence dans Ja nuit du $0 au 31 mars 1993

Le 31 mars 1083 au matin, Ip SEPRA (Service d'Expertise des Phénoménas de
Rentréa Atmosphérique) du CNES & Toulouse étalt contaclé par das dizaines de
témoins (particuliers, brigades de Gendarmérie, aviation civile, observatolres) au sujet
d'un phénoméne aérospatial non Identifié apercu dans de nombreuses régions Je
France. Lo SEPRA metlalt aussitét en place une enquéte qui consistalt a recuelilir les
données des tamolgnages et A en vérifler la corrélation. Les termes employés par les
témoins étaient cohérents et permeliaiant de noter iss dénominateurs oommuns des
témolgnages : observation vers 02 h 10 / 02 h 14 (heure francalse) d'un phSnoméne.
abrospatial traversant le cle! du Nord-Ouest pu Sud-Esl.

La description du phénoméns par les téméins, I'heure d'odservation et la duréa de
calle-cl (de l'ordre de 30 A 60 secondes environ), ont permis de constater qu’
s'agissalt du méme phénoméne observé en différents teux.

Le SEPRA, avec le support des services d’prbitographie du CNES, a procédé 4 css
travaux de corrélation des observations recualllies avec la renirée dans !'atmosphére
Gu Same étage d'une fusée russe ayant mis en orbite le satellite d’observalion
COSMOS 2238. Ce lancement @ ev lieu la 30 mars & 18 h OO TU (20 h 00 heur

francaise). Les données transmises au NES, via la NASA, par l'organtsaticn
&meéricaing NORAD ont permis de reconstituer la trajectolre du 3ame étage montrar

le survol do la France le 34 mars entre OO h 10 et 00 h 12 TU, solt 02 bh 10 ef C2 h12

(heure francalse).

L'ensemble des éléments recuelllls, coordonnés entre eux, montrent la relation de
cause a effet entre les observations et la rentrée dans l'atmosphére du 3éme Silage

coe la fusée russe.

Un cas similaire s’étalt prodult le 5 novemore 1990 a l'occasion du lancemant d’un
satellite de télbcommunications russe GORIZON 21.

Rappelons quien phase de sentrée dans atmosphére, un corps arlificiel est soum!
a un échauffement Important (pouvant aller Jusqu’é 4000°C en fonction de sa vilesse

at de son poids) et génére des phénoménes lumineux. .

)¢ club —

MUSICIAN Stephen Sheering
says he does not believe in fly-
ing saucers — but he claims to
have had a close encounter
with a triangle of mystery
lights.

Mr Sheering, 36, says.he was inex-

Avebury. He says he was one of a
group of about 30 people from all
over the world who congregated at
the top of the hill.

“None of us knew what we went.
there for, it was just a feeling that we
had to go” said Mr Sheering of Pen-
hill, Swindon. *

| “It's not easy to explain what we
saw but a triangle of lights about 40
feet across glided silently past. It
was truly unbelievable and I just
don't know what it-was except that it
was an unidentified flying object. "

He says the strange experience
happened two years ago and the

. lights were also seen by people on
the hill who came from France, Ger-
many and even China.

Mr Sheering, whe ig married with
a daughter, has been a keen UFO
spotter for several years. After the
Sitbury sighting he contacted other
UFO clubs throughout the country
to see if there was an explanation for
the lights.

“No one seems to know what it
was,” he said. “I am not saying it
cule trom another planet or any-
thing like that. It could have been a
stealth bomber but we want to know
what it was,”

Now Mr Sheering and some of his
UFO spotter friends who go out
searching the skies twice a week are
forming a new UFO Wiltshire club
and want to hear from anyone else
who would like to join them.

They plan to have monthly meet-

.ings with guest speakers and night
sky outings.

“Mysterious light triangles are
being spotted all over Europe at the
moment and some people are claim-
ing to have seen occupants,” he said.

“Tt could be one of many things
and our new club aims to carry outa
very | serious effort to solve the mys-
tery.’

Anyone interested in the new UFO
club should ring Stephen on (0793)
729697.

uring 1991, the

“Ah, yes ... they were grey ... and four foor

operaling table. You will remember various

Bui what can it all mean? Surely no one

from a distant universe atiempting to make
conlact humanity? Could i1 be that we
are suffering from a widespread 2Hd-
century oulbreak of contagious mass
hysteria? Or is there something altogether
more sinister going on?

Expert opinian varies dramatically. Some
scientists have dedicated their dives to the
development af a rational explanation: others
have declared themselves cautiously open-
minded. Yel others are convinced that aliens
have actuatly made contact — but that the
world’s governments are desperately trying 10
husk this up. Is this simply paranoia, or does
some truth underlie the latest wave of disturb-
ing stories from the UFO watchers?

For some. there is title doubt, Jokn Keel. a
mainstream: journalist with an interest in weird
phenomena, was one of the first investigators
fo encounter the freakier side of space atiens.
In the mid-Sixties, he began to check out
reports involving UFOs, power failures, and
a winged, half-human

Mothman. His attempts
10 interview witnesses,
however, were impeded
by a series of strange

were interrupted by
static or gibberish. and
sometimes by people
claiming to be atiens
themselves.

Each time he came
up with a theory to

i1 would be immedi-
ately and mystezi-
ously confirmed.
‘Once. he noticed

bom on September 6. Within days, be was

ventually. he talked to peo-
ple wha claimed to have
seen aliens or their ships.
‘On onc occasion, a woman
who had deen regressed
under hypnosis introduced
f herself as an alien called
eel Apol — and predicted the
imminent deaths of Robert Kennedy, Marilyn

Then there is the case of Dr X. a respected
biological scientist living in the South of
France. In the early morning of November 2,
1968, he woke to sce sirange pulsations of
light emitied by a pair of large. bright. disc
shaped objects, silver above and sunset-red
below, several miles away. As he stood. mes-
merised. the two objects coalesced into‘one
and moved towards him, bathing his house in
an unearthly light. He shielded his eves and
after a few moments heard a bang and the craft
vanished into a evanescent vapour. A wound
on his jeft Jeg. incurred several days before,
had, he realised. mysteriously healed.

In another case described by a psychology
professor at the University of Connecticut,

denly she felt she was tumbling head over

speed. Falling from the couch to the floor. she
began to vomit relentlessly — and then found
‘the lump in her dreasi had gone.

There are some who might think such tales
are suspicious in themselves. And there are
plenty who think the whole business of alien

cenain high-ranking government officials.

ypnotic trance.

seems, the ufologisis still

A NEW book by Jenny Randles, The ,
Unexplained: Great Mysteries of the 20th

Here, a Courier writer talks to the author ©

about her views on the paranormal.

THERE ARE people who are receptive
to the notion of the supernatural, and
there are scientists—factions as dis-
tinct and disparate as Campbells and
Macdonalds. Somewhere in between, a

with an interest in the paranormal

‘kindled by her grandmother's psychic

abilities.

“When I tried to get my views across :

N NOVEMBER 1958 Dr and

Mrs M. Moore were driving in

their car through the deserts
of Dakota when suddenly they
looked up and noticed in the sky
a “silvery, cigar-shaped object,
like a giant: windsock,” which
accelerated out of view leaving

behind it a trail of strange purple ~

clouds. Their sighting was
reported to the Flying Saucer
Review and like so many other
similar accounts marked down
as a highly promising incidence
ofa UFO.

Many similar ‘sightings have

even prog dramatic pho-.

tographs of huge saucer or lens
shaped objects ‘in the sky. But
they all look rather ghostly and
suggest something less extrater-
restrial to their origins. Later
analyses showed they were in
fact unusual clouds called lentic-
ular clouds. These have an aston-

ishing resemblarice to a collosal ©

flying saucer, often with circular.
rings around them and occasion-
ally luminous if the sun is shin-
ing from behind.: -

Lenticular clouds come from
mountains but are fairly unusu-
al. Normally winds passing over
mountains lose their strength
and become weaker on ‘the lee-
ward side. But occasionally
something strange happens —
the air on the leeward side
bounces up and down like a bob-

Scotland has become
a focus point for visi-

Falkirk Town’ Hall on

- Jurie 30, will attract
more than 700 people
.from all over the world.

| UFO buffs who claim to

UFO i issue oo

bing wave with nowhere to go,
It's caused by air on top of the’
mountain pushing down on the
“bottom of the leeward side and
“squashing” the air there. If the
air is also moist, then every time
the air bobs up a lenticular cloud
is formed.

Apart from their shape they .
behave even more UFO-like

EDITOR - I am ‘writing to you to express my
concern over a report in last week's
Mablethorpe and, Sutton News under the head,
‘UFO Watch not a good idea’, The information
in this article was supplied by Jenny Randel an
expert on the UFO phenomena. Foe

She says she did not think the recent idea of a
UFO watch was a good idea? “It feeds the
sceptics’ theory of someone sitting on a hill
waiting for little green men."" What Miss Randel
didn’t mention in the newspaper, however, was
that on’her phone in UFO Call she ends with the
words ‘Keep those cyes on the skies". ;

Obviously she does not mean the people of
Mablethorpe and Sutton-on-Sea. Perhaps she
:Should be saying: “Keep those eyes on me ['m
the only one that knows what I'm talking about.”*

.:, It is possible that Miss Randel’s ego is much,

larger than her capacity for getting the facts

correct? Or does she suffer from memory loss? -

“After ail, it was only three wecks ago that in a
telephone conversation with my wife, she
wished Skywatch every success and said she
would be only too pleased to exchange
information with the group.

I can only assume that since then some
sinister agent has been at work and persuaded
‘Miss Randel to change fact into fantasy.

sighting, it occurred to me
that perhaps our alien
friends were looking for
work down here. 137

But when they realised wf)

hada Tory Government, they’

- knew they had no chance and

shot off elsewhere. They.

must have problems the same
as we on Earth - U.F.O.S:
(Unemployed From Outer
Space). - W. McArthur,

‘The Times, London

S MAR 1994
More and more people claim to have been abducted i in spaceships. What lies behind it?

yee ee

Re of this ‘paper probably -

do not routinely :read The
People. So they would have
missed an extraordinary recent story, -

about seven people who claimed to.

have been abducted fram their homes
by aliens, taken on board spaceships, .
and then subjected to a series of
humiliating sexual assaults: .

-These stories are new to. this
country, but are. common:.in the

- United States. According to journalist

Jim: Schnabel, whose. fascinating
book Dark White: Aliens, Abductions

. and the UFO Obsession has just been
_ published here by Hamish Hamilton, .
the first case surfaced in the 1960s. .

Since then the topic has- become a
craze, with numerous journals and
conventions devoted to these U
abductions, as well as support gi
for the alleged victims, known ‘as
UFO abductees. .

. The stories are so bizarre that the
instinctive reaction is to dismiss them
as the delusions of individuals suffer-

ing from severe mental iliness. How- -

ever, the “victims” are. not -usually
mentally ill in the sense of, suffering
from a psychotic illness, nor are they
drug or alcohol: abusers. A recent
paper in the prestigious Journal of

Abnormal Psycholo, esate them -

as “solid representa’
can middle class”.

ives of the Ameri-

Most of those claiming t tohave been :

abducted by aliens say the experience.

began during sleep, and was associat- ,
ed with extreme emotional states. The:::
. Victim may recall vivid,- uriexpected-:

and frightening internal sensations,

such as terror,-visual hallucinations, -.
choking sensations and other abnor- ©
mal sensations affecting many Parts

of the body.

related to brief episodes of epileptic
activity in the temporal lobe of the:

>

~ and
Science

Sexual |
fantasy

fiction

brain, also implicated in the genesis!
of complex hallucinations such as.
religious visions. Others have been
linked to sleep-related disturbances:
such as sleep paralysis. This not:
uncommon phenomenon occurs in
people either when going to sleep or
on wakening, and causes muscle

_ paralysis and frightening : sensations ;

such ¢ as choking. -

These mechanisms may be suffi
..clent to account for the abnor:
“inal sensations that: herald al
UFO experience, but do not account!

“for the fully formed stories of being |
:transported to- alien spacecraft. To
.. understand those one needs to consid- i 4
* er the victims background and pre

existing beliefs... - -
He or she probably also suifers |
from what are known as dissociative '

“+ disorders, in which. the normal inte",
" Neuropsychologists have. ‘sugges -
ed that their experiences may be -

gration .of thought,: feelings and’
experience into memory and con-:
sciousness is interrupted. A person's ;
ability to dissociate is determined by;

many things -~ childhood abuse and
trauma are current focuses of atten-
tion. The result may be an extreme
example of something we all possess
— the capacity for intense self-
absorption. during which we lose
track of time. Those who experience
dissociative phenomena are often
highly suggestible. Many so-called

:. abductees only piece together that

they have been kidnapped by aliens
some time after thé abnormal experi-
ence, often with the “help” of ill-
advised hypnotic regression. Thus the
UFO story may be an attempt to

_ provide an explanation for the gaps in
, “memory that result from dissociation.
One must also consider the role of .

fantasy. Some accounts may be vivid
dreams, in which fantasy and reality
are easily confused. Others are relat-
ed to sexual fantasy. Most of the

stories of abductees were of a disturb- _

ing sexual nature involving abuse or
humiliation. One related in The
People included medical evidence of

real rape. Others describe medical

experimentation including being op-

erated on by aliens who frequently -
remove eggs from the subject’s womb
or inseminate them.

The final thread is found in the
background beliefs and culture of the
subject. The study carried in the

current Journal of Abnormal Psy +

chology reported that those with the

most intense UFO experiences shared <

a similar range of pre-existing beliefs.
Those ranged from the only mildly
unusual, such as telepathy or astrolo-
gy, to the more bizarre, such as
channelling, or spontaneous combus-
tion, Also shared was aconspiratorial:
view of the world. Those active in the
UFO movement overlap with those
who spend their time deriving elabo-
rate conspiracy theories of the Kenne-
dy assassination.

IN these otherwise incompre-
hensible stories can be under-
stood only by bringing
together the sufferers’ internal and
external worlds. People who become
convinced that they have been ab-
ducted by aliens are those who
already believe in the paranormal in
general and UFOs/aliens in particu-.
lar. They then experience vivid,

terrifying experiences such as occur
in sleep and dreams. Perhaps because
of previous trauma, they also have the
capacity for intense introspection,
which may lead to- gaps in their
memories. These sensory experiences
and subsequent memory gaps are

then. elaborated, sometimes under .
hypnosis. The consequence is the’.

mixture of sexual fantasy and science
fiction that constitutes their final.
belief. . '

DR SIMON WESSELY

@ The author is sentor lecturer in psycho-
logical medicine at the Maudsley
Hospital.

by DAVE FOX

“UFO experts reckon
reports of a ‘flying
Saucer’ above —
Frodsham and.-” jj
Runcorn are {

. more than a flight

-of fancy. .

There have been a spate

. Of sightings of a strange
cigar-shaped craft over the
‘past few weeks,

Members of the British
UFO Research Associa-
lion are taking them seri-
ously and are now carrying

~sout an in-depth investiga-
‘tion . °
~ Latest to report a sighting is
74-year-old former lorry driver
Mr Tom Wade, who lives in
Clifton Drive at Frodsham. ~
" Weelchair-bound Mr Wade
saw the UFO out of an upstairs
window at 9.30pm at night and
it has left him baffled.
. ‘At first I thought the moon
was very bright. | pulled the
curtains apart and realised it
was not the moon but an abject
with lights along the side.

“Le was lit up with blue, gold

and orange colours along the:
side. So,
‘At first it appeared to be sta-

tionary and then it moved off -

towards ICI Rocksavage
—Works. It was strange because-
there was no sound, . ’
 -Then it swung sharply round
and headed towards thé Kast-
ner Kellner towers. After about
“three minutes it flew off in the
direction of Liverpool.’ |
Added Mr Wade: ‘It was not
an aircraft or a helicopter. The
way it moved was comptetcly
different. .
‘I've always thought there
might be something in UFO re-
ports. After seeing this I believe
they do exist." °
An investigator from the
* British UFO Research Associa-
tion has taken a statement from

is

Mr Wade as part of inquiries
into over 70 recent eye-witness
accounts of UFO sightings.
Said association spokesman
Philip Mantle: ‘Strangely we
“havé had an awful jot of similar
_Teports from the Wirral and

. Chester area and also from over,

‘on the East Coast around Sear-
borough.

‘They've all come over about
six weeks and a fot of them are
of a very similar nature.

‘At the moment we-have not
been able to come up with an
explanation and that means
we're keeping an open'mind
about it.

‘Plenty of people will have
seen the film Close Encounters
ofthe Third Kind and | have no

loubt personally there are cralt

from another world."

VIDEO MYSTERY OF THE UFO THAT'S BAFFLING THE MEN FROM THE MINISTRY

CATCH OF THE DAY: The Fishermans video ofthe drain ng, silent object — but is ita UFO o ran image rf he @ camcorder's iris? The MoD is still investigating '
24 ,

it came 2 from outer

GUILTY? The iris of the MC6
Panasonic camcorder.

!

By NICK FIELDING and
RICHARD HELLER
‘HELLO, get me the

: Defence Secretary! I've :

just seen a flying = -
saucer full of Martians

firing gamma-ray guns _

over the Norfolk
Broads.’

‘Thank you, sir, Could you °
please put the details on this
official form?

It sounds like a seript for The ©

Men From The Ministry. But no. .

It happened to three responsible
citizens who genuinely believed

they had sighted an Unidentified

Flying Object.

On October 23 last year, Mark
Wilkins, Bill Deuters and
Stephen Farrow, from
Dagenham, Essex, went fishing
on Rollesby Broad in Norfolk.
And, as ustial, they took Mark’s
Panasonic MC6 camcorder to film
their catches.

That day, though, the wind made

them abandon the trip at 3 pm. ~

But as they unloaded their gear,
Steve suddenly shouted and

- pointed at ‘this bright light a

long way away’, he recails.

Bill pointed the camcorder at it
— and could not believe what he
saw, ‘Through the lens it looked
like an intensely glowing object.
At first it was near the horizon,
It was coming straight at us over
our heads. Ag I zoomed in it
turned into an intense diamond.’
Bill stopped filming after about
six seconds — and then all three
saw & very bright light move over
them, south to north. They
thought it might be a military
aircraft, but it made no noise.
The trio thought no more about
it until they piayed the tape
back. It had captured a bright

object which turned into a
distinct diamond shape. The
images looked so authentic that
they decided to send them to the
MoD.

Was it friend or foe? Shouid the

- nation’s guns be pointed

skywards?

Their film went to the official at
the nerve centre of our defence
against extra-terrestrials — Mr
N. G. Pope, of the Secretariat
(Air Staff) 2a, :

Horizon

Mr Pope responded to 258 reports
of UFO sightings last year. Most
were quickly explained as
satellites, aircraft, weather
balloons, lightning, meteorites —
or hoaxes. But not this one.

‘His brief ts very precise. ‘The _
Ministry of Defence's only

“concern with UFOs is to

establish whether or not there is

any threat to the security of the
United Kingdom. To date, we are
not aware of any evidence that
would indicate the existence of
such a threat,’ he told the three
men soberly.

Yet, later in the same letter of
January T, Mr Pope could not
restrain his enthusiasm: “Your
video is intriguing and certainly
one of the most interesting I
have seen. I have to say that no
explanation springs to mind.’
But before he could scramble our
fighter defences, Mr Pope had to
follow Ministry procedures. ‘It
would help, he continued, ‘to
have further details, so I have
attached a copy of the form we
use to record details of sightings.”

Allowing another month or two ~

to set up & Ministerial committee
to study it, the little green chaps

- would now be in Downing Street.

Fortunately, the three men also
sent their video to The Mail on

Sunday. And we rushed it to
experts for analysis. At the
sophisticated laboratories of
Network Security Management in
Maytair, Jon Walklin, of the
company’s forensic video section,
was mystified.

‘As far as I can determine, it _
depicts a genuine object,’ he said.

Briefing

The Meteorclogical Office and
London Weather Centre said it
was not a weather balloon. The
RAF and civil aviation authorities
eliminated military aircraft and
North Sea, helicopters.

‘Then Great Yarmouth coastguard
told us that the night after the’
sighting they had a call ten miles
from Rollesby Broad reporting a

. bright flare.

Should we fight or flee? Why stay
calm when it costs so little to
panic? We consulted more experts.

Nerves were o steadied by Bernie

Dot Forward, a senior inspector at the
_ Aircraft Accident Investigation

Branch at Farnborough, which

f° examined the Pan Am jet after ,

the Lockerbie disaster. He -
pronounced the image to be an
aircraft. Colleagues speculated -
that it could be a prism effect
caused by the camcorder lens. .
And that was echoed by Dr Peter

- Andrews, of the Royal

Observatory, Cambridge.

Even Philip Mantle, director of
investigations for the British’
UFO Research Association,
thought the image was either an
aircraft or an optical effect.
Finally, we sent the video to
Martin Hanson, of Panasonic.
‘Looks like a camera iris image to
me,’ he said, A colleague found
an iris of the type fitted te model

: : / -MC6, When open, the iris —
EYES IN THE SI SKY: From the teft, Bill Deuter, Stephen Farrow and Mark Wilkina = Picture: HUGH PINNEY

which regulates the light going
into the camcorder — created a
diamond shape.

‘The Mail on Sunday believes this
explains the startling imege.
Somehow, the iris was reflected
on to the back of the lens and
filmed. But that still leaves a
mystery of the dazzling light the
men saw. And Mr Pope is still
pursuing the matter.

As far as the Ministry is
concerned, there still might be
Something Out There.

A colleague said last week: ‘We
cannot explain it. If there is a
possibility we can identify it and
What aircraft are invoived, we
He has promised us ‘a full
briefing’ this week.

Until that happens, observers of
mysterious objects are requested

CATCH OF THE DAY: The fisherman’ 's video of the dorating, silent object — but
